The definition of the auxiliary-rescaling map
\pi_{f}^{0}:{}_{2}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{4,\tau_{0}}^{1}\rightarrow{}_{2}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{4}^{S_{0,1}^{1}}

Finally, there is a free
\mathbb{Z}_{k+1}
-action on the moduli space
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}
generated by the map

\kappa:{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}\rightarrow{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}},

(4.37)

which cyclically permutes the labels of the boundary marked points. Concretely,
\kappa
changes the label
z_{i}
to
z_{i+1}
if
0\leq i\leq k-1
, and
z_{k}
to
z_{0}
. It can be shown that this
\mathbb{Z}_{k+1}
-action is properly discontinuous. For a similar (in fact, almost identical) situation, see
[
28
]
, Lemma 12.

Remark 37
.

Similar auxiliary moduli spaces as
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}
,
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{S_{i}^{1}}
,
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{S_{i,i+1}^{1}}
and
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}
considered above also play important roles in Ganatra’s construction of cyclic open-closed maps
[
28
]
. The main difference between our set up and his is that the marked points
z_{0},\cdots,z_{k}\in\partial D
there are treated as punctures, and therefore equipped with strip-like ends (and Floer data) for the purpose of constructing closed-open maps. Here, as we have mentioned, they are regarded as marked points on the Moore loops, so one can study the corresponding forgetful maps. These forgetful maps will be important for the construction of cyclic invariant Kuranishi structures in Section
4.3
.

In order to study the Floer theory associated to the domains in
{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}
, we need the corresponding notion of Floer data. Note that by identifying an element of
{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}
with a half-cylinder
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times S^{1}
, we can assign each point
p_{i}
with an
s
-coordinate
(p_{i})_{s}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, and a
t
-coordinate
(p_{i})_{t}\in S^{1}
.

Definition 38
.

A Floer datum for an element of
{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}
consists of the following:

•

A positive cylindrical end which is compatible with the asymptotic marker, namely an embedding

\varepsilon^{+}:[0,\infty)\times S^{1}\rightarrow S,\textrm{ }(s,t)\mapsto\left(s+(p_{l})_{s}+\eta,t\right)

(4.38)

for some
\eta>0
.

•

A sub-closed 1-form
\nu_{S}\in\Omega^{1}(S)
such that
\nu_{S}\equiv 0
near
\partial S
and
(\varepsilon^{+})^{\ast}\nu_{S}=dt
.

•

A domain-dependent Hamiltonian
H_{S}:S\times M\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfying

(\varepsilon^{+})^{\ast}H_{S}=H_{t}

(4.39)

for some
H_{t}\in\mathcal{H}(M)
, and

H_{S}\equiv 0\textrm{ near }\partial S.

(4.40)

•

A domain-dependent almost complex structure
J_{S}:S\times\mathit{TM}\rightarrow\mathit{TM}
such that

(\varepsilon^{+})^{\ast}J_{S}=J_{t}

(4.41)

for some
J_{t}\in\mathcal{J}(M)
, and

J_{S}\equiv J_{M}\textrm{ near }\partial S,

(4.42)

where
J_{M}
is the almost complex structure fixed in Section
3.4
.

In a similar fashion, we can define what it means for a Floer datum on the auxiliary moduli spaces
{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}
and
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}
. We will inductively choose the Floer data on the compactified moduli spaces
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}
so that they satisfy certain consistency conditions. In order to do so, we first choose a Floer datum on the auxiliary moduli space
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}
so that

•

The Floer datum on
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}
is
\mathbb{Z}_{k+1}
-equivariant under the cyclic permutation
\kappa
.

•

On the boundary stratum
{}_{l-1}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}\subset\partial{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}
, the Floer datum is conformally equivalent to the one pulled back from
{}_{l-2}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}
via the forgetful map
\pi_{j}^{S^{1}}
.

Definition 39
.

A Cohen-Ganatra Floer datum is a an inductive sequence of choices, for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
l\in\mathbb{N}
, of Floer data for every representative of
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}
in the sense of Definition
38
, which vary smoothly over the moduli spaces, and is required to satisfy the following :

(i)

The choice of Floer datum on any boundary stratum should agree with the inductively chosen datum along the boundary stratum for which we have already picked the data.

(ii)

Near the boundary strata in (
4.30
), the Floer data are conformally equivalent to the ones obtained by puling back from
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}
via the forgetful maps
\pi_{j}
.

(iii)

On the codimension 1 loci
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S_{i}^{1}}\subset{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}
, where
p_{1}
points at
z_{i}
, the Floer datum should agree with the pullback by
\tau_{i}
of the existing Floer datum on
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}
.

Proposition 40
.

A Cohen-Ganatra Floer datum exists.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that the choices of Floer data at each stage is contractible, and for a suitable inductive order, the conditions imposed on Floer data at various strata do not contradict each other. For a similar situation, see
[
28
]
, Proposition 10.
∎

Fix a Cohen-Ganatra Floer datum, and a 1-periodic orbit
x
of
X_{H_{t}}
, which is a generator of the symplectic cochain complex
\mathit{SC}^{\ast}(M)
. Let
\pi_{2}(M,x,L)
be the set of homotopy classes of maps
u:S\rightarrow M
with boundary on
L
and asymptotic to
x
at the puncture
\zeta
. There is a natural map
\partial:\pi_{2}(M,x,L)\rightarrow H_{1}(L;\mathbb{Z})
defined by sending
[u]\in\pi_{2}(M,x,L)
to the homology class
[u(\partial S)]\in H_{1}(L;\mathbb{Z})
. For any
\ring{\beta}\in\pi_{2}(M,x,L)
, define the
Cohen-Ganatra moduli space

{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})

(4.43)

to be the space of pairs

\left((S;z_{0},\cdots,z_{k},p_{1},\cdots,p_{l};\ell),u\right),

(4.44)

where
(S;z_{0},\cdots,z_{k},p_{1},\cdots,p_{l};\ell)\in{}_{l}\mathcal{R}^{1}_{k+1}
, and the map
u:S\rightarrow M
satisfies

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(du-X_{H_{S}}\otimes\nu_{S})^{0,1}=0,\\
u(\partial S)\subset L,\\
\lim_{s\rightarrow\infty}(\varepsilon^{+})^{\ast}u(s,\cdot)=x,\\
{[u]}=\ring{\beta},\end{array}\right.

(4.45)

where the
(0,1)
-part in the Floer equation is taken with respect to
J_{S}
.

Among the Cohen-Ganatra moduli spaces
{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})
, there is one special case that will be particularly important for us, namely the moduli space

\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}(e_{M},L,\beta),

(4.46)

which is defined using domains without the auxiliary interior marked points
p_{1},\cdots,p_{l}
, and the asymptotic condition at the positive puncture is given by the minimum of a
C^{2}
-small Morse function
f:M^{\mathit{in}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined in the interior
M^{\mathit{in}}
of the Liouville domain
\overline{M}
. In this case, the homotopy class
\ring{\beta}
can be identified with a class
\beta\in\pi_{2}(M,L)
under the isomorphism
\pi_{2}(M,e_{M},L)\cong\pi_{2}(M,L)
, which explains the notation.

Given
x\in\mathit{SC}^{\ast}(M)
and
\ring{\beta}\in\pi_{2}(M,x,L)
, in a similar fashion as above, we can define the moduli spaces

{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta}),

(4.47)

{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta}),\textrm{ }0\leq i\leq k,

(4.48)

{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta}),\textrm{ }1\leq j\leq l-1,

(4.49)

which parametrize the same maps
u:S\rightarrow M
as in (
4.45
), but with the domains in the moduli spaces
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}
,
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}
and
{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}
, respectively.

When defining (
4.48
) (and its compactification), we need to choose Floer data on the moduli spaces
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}
. In this case, there is an oriented diffeomorphism

\tau_{i}:{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}\rightarrow{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{0}}^{1}

(4.50)

induced by cyclic permutations of the boundary labels. The Floer datum for
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}
shall be chosen so that it coincides with the pullback of the Floer datum on
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1,\tau_{0}}^{1}
by (
4.50
).

The Gromov compactification
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})
of the Cohen-Ganatra moduli space is an admissible K-space. Its detailed description will be postponed to the next subsection.

4.3
Kuranishi structures on compactified moduli spaces

The purpose of this subsection is to state Theorem
44
, where we describe the compactifications of the moduli spaces
\mathcal{R}_{k+1,\vartheta}(L,\beta)
and
{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})
introduced in Sections
4.1
and
4.2
as admissible K-spaces. We start with the following lemma which summarizes the basic properties of these moduli spaces.

Lemma 41
.

The following properties of the moduli spaces hold.

(i)

When
\beta=0
, the moduli space
\mathcal{R}_{k+1,\vartheta}(L,0)
consists of constant maps for every
k\geq 3
, and
\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}(e_{M},L,0)
consists of constant maps for every
k\geq 0
.

(ii)

If
\theta_{M}(\partial\ring{\beta})+|A_{H_{t}}(x)|<0
, then
{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})=\emptyset
, where
H_{t}\in\mathcal{H}(M)
is a fixed choice of some admissible Hamiltonian, which serves as part of our Floer datum, and

A_{H_{t}}(x)=-\int_{S^{1}}x^{\ast}\theta_{M}+\int_{S^{1}}H_{t}(x(t))dt

(4.51)

is the action of the orbit
x
of
X_{H_{t}}
.

(iii)

For every
k\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\mathcal{R}_{k+1,\vartheta}(L,\beta)=\emptyset\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{R}_{2,\vartheta}(L,\beta)=\emptyset.

(4.52)

For every
k,l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have

{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})=\emptyset\Leftrightarrow{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})=\emptyset.

(4.53)

Moreover, for every
c>0
and fixed
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, the sets

\left\{\beta\in\pi_{2}(M,L)|\mathcal{R}_{2,\vartheta}(L,\beta)\neq\emptyset,\theta_{M}(\partial\beta)<c\right\},

(4.54)

\left\{\ring{\beta}\in\pi_{2}(M,x,L)\left|{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})\neq\emptyset,\theta_{M}(\partial\ring{\beta})<c\right.\right\}

(4.55)

are both finite.

Proof.

(i) is obvious for
\mathcal{R}_{k+1,\vartheta}(L,0)
. For any map
u:(S,\partial S)\rightarrow(M,L)
parametrized by
\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}(e_{M},L,\beta)
, the removable singularity theorem for pseudoholomorphic maps implies that there exists a
J_{M}
-holomorphic map
\bar{u}:(D,\partial D)\rightarrow(M,L)
, with
\left[\bar{u}\right]=\beta
and
\bar{u}(0)
mapped to the minimum of the
C^{2}
-small Morse function
f:M^{\mathit{in}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
. If
\bar{u}
is constant, so must be
u
.

(ii) is an energy estimate. For any element (which is represented by a pair (
4.44
)) of
{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}(L,x,\ring{\beta})
, we have

0\leq E^{\mathit{geom}}(u)=\int_{S}\frac{1}{2}||du-X_{H_{S}}\otimes\nu_{S}||^{2}\leq E^{\mathit{top}}(u)=\theta_{M}(\partial\ring{\beta})+A_{H_{t}}(x),

(4.56)

where
E^{\mathit{geom}}(u)
and
E^{\mathit{top}}(u)
are the geometric and the topological energies of
u
, respectively (cf.
[
3
]
, Section 7.2). Note that in the above computations, we have used the fact that
H_{S}\equiv 0
near
\partial S
.

(iii) is a consequence of Gromov compactness.
∎

To describe the compactifications of the moduli spaces
{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})
,
{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})
,
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta})
and
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})
introduced in Section
4.2
, we need a slight variation of the notion of decorated rooted ribbon trees.

Definition 42
.

A decorated rooted ribbon tree with a single puncture is a triple
(T,\ring{B},v_{0})
such that the tree
T
satisfies (i)-(v) in the definition of a decorated rooted ribbon tree, but now there is a distinguished interior vertex
v_{0}\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)
which is called a puncture. The conditions imposed on the map
B:C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\rightarrow\pi_{2}(M,L)
in Definition
33
, (vi) and (vii) are replaced with the following:

(vi)’

There exists a map
\ring{B}:C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\rightarrow\pi_{2}(M,x,L)
, such that its restrictions to
C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\setminus\{v_{0}\}
and
\{v_{0}\}
give rise to maps
C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\setminus\{v_{0}\}\rightarrow\pi_{2}(M,L)
and
\{v_{0}\}\rightarrow\pi_{2}(M,x,L)
, respectively, where
x
is a 1-periodic orbit of
X_{H_{t}}
. For every
v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)
, either
d\theta_{M}\left(\ring{B}(v)\right)>0
or
\ring{B}(v)=0
. Note that for
v=v_{0}
, this means that
x
is a constant orbit, so
\ring{B}(v_{0})
can be regarded as a class in
\pi_{2}(M,L)
.

(vii)’

Every
v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\setminus\{v_{0}\}
with
\ring{B}(v)=0
has valency at least
3
. When
x
is a constant orbit, we also require that
v_{0}
has valency at least
3
if
\ring{B}(v_{0})=0
in
\pi_{2}(M,L)
.

Note that when
x
is a constant orbit, this reduces to the notion of a decorated rooted ribbon tree. For
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
\ring{\beta}\in\pi_{2}(M,x,L)
, denote by
\mathcal{G}(k+1,\ring{\beta})
the set of decorated rooted ribbon trees with a single puncture
(T,\ring{B},v_{0})
, such that
\#C_{0,\mathit{ext}}(T)=k+1
and
\sum_{v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)}\ring{B}(v)=\ring{\beta}
.

We also have the following extension of a reduction of decorated rooted ribbon trees (cf.
[
34
]
, Definition 7.19).

Definition 43
.

Let
(T,\ring{B},v_{0})\in\mathcal{G}(k+1,\ring{\beta})
and
e\in C_{1,\mathit{int}}(T)
, with
v_{0},v_{1}
being vertices of
e
. By collapsing
e
to a new vertex
v_{01}
, we get another
(T^{\prime},\ring{B}^{\prime},v_{0})\in\mathcal{G}(k+1,\ring{\beta})
such that

C_{0}(T^{\prime})=\left(C_{0}(T)\setminus\{v_{0},v_{1}\}\right)\cup\{v_{01}\},

(4.57)

C_{1}(T^{\prime})=C_{1}(T)\setminus\{e\},

(4.58)

\ring{B}^{\prime}(v)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\ring{B}(v)&v\neq v_{01},\\
\ring{B}(v_{0})+\ring{B}(v_{1})&v=v_{01}.\end{array}\right.

(4.59)

An element of
\mathcal{G}(k+1,\ring{\beta})
which can be obtained from
(T,\ring{B},v_{0})\in\mathcal{G}(k+1,\ring{\beta})
by repeating the above procedure is called a reduction of
(T,\ring{B},v_{0})
.

Let
X
be an admissible K-space. For
r\in\mathbb{N}
, denoted by
\widehat{S}_{r}X
the normalized codimension
r
corner of
X
. When
r=1
, we shall also write
\partial X
for the normalized boundary.

From now on, let
\varepsilon>0
be chosen as in the beginning of Section
3.4
, so that every non-constant
J_{M}
-holomorphic disc bounded by
L
has area at most
2\varepsilon
. Take
U\in\mathbb{N}
so that
\varepsilon(U-1)\geq|A_{H_{t}}(x)|
.

Theorem 44
.

For every
k,m,l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and
P=\{m\}
or
[m,m+1]
, there exist the following data.

(i)

(Moduli spaces) Compact, oriented, admissible K-spaces (when
l=0
, the moduli spaces (
4.63
), (
4.64
) and (
4.65
) are empty)

\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}(L,\beta;P),\textrm{ where }\beta\in\pi_{2}(M,L)\textrm{ and }\theta_{M}(\partial\beta)<(m-k+1)\varepsilon,

(4.60)

\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;P),\textrm{ where }\beta\in\pi_{2}(M,L)\textrm{ and }\theta_{M}(\partial\beta)<(m-k+1)\varepsilon,

(4.61)

{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P),\textrm{ where }\ring{\beta}\in\pi_{2}(M,x,L)\textrm{ and }\theta_{M}(\partial\ring{\beta})<(m-k-U)\varepsilon,

(4.62)

{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P),\textrm{ where }\ring{\beta}\in\pi_{2}(M,x,L)\textrm{ and }\theta_{M}(\partial\ring{\beta})<(m-k-U)\varepsilon,

(4.63)

{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P),\textrm{ where }\ring{\beta}\in\pi_{2}(M,x,L)\textrm{ and }\theta_{M}(\partial\ring{\beta})<(m-k-U)\varepsilon,

(4.64)

{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P),\textrm{ where }\ring{\beta}\in\pi_{2}(M,x,L)\textrm{ and }\theta_{M}(\partial\ring{\beta})<(m-k-U)\varepsilon,

(4.65)

whose underlying topological spaces are
P\times\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}(L,\beta)
,
P\times\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta)
,
P\times{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})
,
P\times{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})
,
P\times{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta})
, and
P\times{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})
, respectively. Dimensions of these K-spaces are

\dim\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;P)=\dim\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}(L,\beta;P)=\mu(\beta)+n+k-2+\dim P,

(4.66)

\dim{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)=\mu(\ring{\beta})+k+2l+\mathit{CZ}(x)+\dim P,

(4.67)

\begin{split}\dim{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)&=\dim{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)=\dim{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\\
&=\mu(\ring{\beta})+k+2l-1+\mathit{CZ}(x)+\dim P,\end{split}

(4.68)

where
\mathit{CZ}(x)
is the Conley-Zehnder index of
x
.

(ii)

(Evaluation maps) Corner-stratified strongly smooth maps

\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R},P}:\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}(L,\beta;P)\rightarrow P\times L^{k+1},

(4.69)

\mathit{ev}_{\vartheta}^{\mathcal{R},P}:\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;P)\rightarrow P\times L^{k+2},

(4.70)

{}_{l}\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R},P}:{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\rightarrow P\times L^{k+1},

(4.71)

{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R},P}:{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\rightarrow P\times L^{k+1},

(4.72)

{}_{l-1}\mathit{ev}^{S^{1},P}:{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\rightarrow P\times L^{k+1},

(4.73)

{}_{l-1}\mathit{ev}_{i}^{\mathcal{R},P}:{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\rightarrow P\times L^{k+1},

(4.74)

such that their underlying set-theoretic maps are
\mathit{id}_{P}\times\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R}}
,
\mathit{id}_{P}\times\mathit{ev}_{\vartheta}^{\mathcal{R}}
,
\mathit{id}_{P}\times{}_{l}\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R}}
,
\mathit{id}_{P}\times{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R}}
,
\mathit{id}_{P}\times{}_{l-1}\mathit{ev}^{S^{1}}
, and
\mathit{id}_{P}\times{}_{l-1}\mathit{ev}_{i}^{\mathcal{R}}
, respectively. Moreover, the maps

(\mathit{id}_{P}\times\mathit{pr}_{0})\circ\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R},P}:\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}(L,\beta;P)\rightarrow P\times L,

(4.75)

(\mathit{id}_{P}\times\mathit{pr}_{0})\circ\mathit{ev}_{\vartheta}^{\mathcal{R},P}:\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;P)\rightarrow P\times L,

(4.76)

(\mathit{id}_{P}\times\mathit{pr}_{0})\circ{}_{l}\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R},P}:{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\rightarrow P\times L,

(4.77)

(\mathit{id}_{P}\times\mathit{pr}_{0})\circ{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R},P}:{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\rightarrow P\times L,

(4.78)

(\mathit{id}_{P}\times\mathit{pr}_{0})\circ\mathit{ev}^{S^{1},P}:{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\rightarrow P\times L,

(4.79)

(\mathit{id}_{P}\times\mathit{pr}_{0})\circ\mathit{ev}_{i}^{\mathcal{R},P}:{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\rightarrow P\times L

(4.80)

are corner-stratified weak submersions, where
\mathit{pr}_{0}:L^{k+1}\rightarrow L
is the projection to the first factor.

(iii)

(Energy zero part) An isomorphism of admissible Kuranishi structures

\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,0;P)\cong P\times L\times D^{k-2}

(4.81)

for every
k\geq 2
, so that
\mathit{ev}_{\vartheta}^{\mathcal{R},P}:\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,0;P)\rightarrow P\times L^{k+2}
coincides with
\mathit{pr}_{P}\times(\mathit{pr}_{L})^{k+2}
, where
\mathit{pr}_{P}
is the projection to
P
and
\mathit{pr}_{L}
is the projection to
L
. Here, the disc
D^{k-2}
on the right-hand side of (
4.81
) is given by the Stasheff cell.

(iv)

(Compatibility at boundaries) Orientation-preserving isomorphisms of admissible K-spaces:

\partial\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;\{m\})\cong\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+k_{2}=k+1\\
1\leq i\leq k_{1}+1\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}=\beta\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta_{1};\{m\})\textrm{ }{{}_{i}\times_{0}}\textrm{ }\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+1}(L,\beta_{2};\{m\}),

(4.82)

\begin{split}\partial{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};\{m\})&\cong\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+k_{2}=k+1\\
1\leq i\leq k_{1}\\
\ring{\beta}_{1}+\beta_{2}=\ring{\beta}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{2}}{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta}_{1};\{m\})\textrm{ }{{}_{i}\times_{0}}\textrm{ }\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+1}(L,\beta_{2};\{m\})\\
&\sqcup\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+k_{2}=k+1\\
1\leq i\leq k_{1}\\
\beta_{1}+\ring{\beta}_{2}=\ring{\beta}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{3}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}(L,\beta_{1};\{m\})\textrm{ }{{}_{i}\times_{0}}\textrm{ }{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{1}_{k_{2}+1}(x,L,\ring{\beta}_{2};\{m\})\\
&\sqcup\bigsqcup_{0\leq j\leq l}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{4,j}}{}_{j}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x,y_{j};\{m\})\times{}_{l-j}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(y_{j},L,\ring{\beta};\{m\})\\
&\sqcup\bigsqcup_{1\leq j\leq l-1}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{5}}{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};\{m\})\\
&\sqcup(-1)^{\varepsilon_{6}}{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};\{m\}),\end{split}

(4.83)

\begin{split}&\partial\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;[m,m+1])\cong(-1)^{\varepsilon_{7}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;\{m\})\sqcup(-1)^{\varepsilon_{8}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;\{m+1\})\\
&\sqcup\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+k_{2}=k+1\\
1\leq i\leq k_{1}+1\\
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}=\beta\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{9}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta_{1};[m,m+1])\textrm{ }{{}_{i}\times_{0}}\textrm{ }\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+1}(L,\beta_{2};[m,m+1]),\end{split}

(4.84)

\begin{split}&\partial{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};[m,m+1])\\
&\cong(-1)^{\varepsilon_{10}}\partial{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};\{m\})\sqcup(-1)^{\varepsilon_{11}}\partial{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};\{m+1\})\\
&\sqcup\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+k_{2}=k+1\\
1\leq i\leq k_{1}\\
\ring{\beta}_{1}+\beta_{2}=\ring{\beta}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{12}}{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta}_{1};[m,m+1])\textrm{ }{{}_{i}\times_{0}}\textrm{ }\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+1}(L,\beta_{2};[m,m+1])\\
&\sqcup\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+k_{2}=k+1\\
1\leq i\leq k_{1}\\
\beta_{1}+\ring{\beta}_{2}=\ring{\beta}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{13}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}(L,\beta_{1};[m,m+1])\textrm{ }{{}_{i}\times_{0}}\textrm{ }{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+1}(x,L,\ring{\beta}_{2};[m,m+1])\\
&\sqcup\bigsqcup_{0\leq j\leq l}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{14,j}}{}_{j}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x,y_{j};[m,m+1])\times{}_{l-j}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(y_{j},L,\ring{\beta};[m,m+1])\\
&\sqcup\bigsqcup_{1\leq j\leq l-1}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{15}}{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};[m,m+1])\sqcup(-1)^{\varepsilon_{16}}{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};[m,m+1]),\end{split}

(4.85)

where the notation
{{}_{i}\times_{0}}
in the above is an abbreviation for the fiber product
{{}_{\mathit{ev}_{i}}\times_{\mathit{ev}_{0}}}
, with
\mathit{ev}_{i}
given by the composition
(\mathit{id}_{P}\times\mathit{pr}_{i})\circ\mathit{ev}^{\bullet,P}
, where
\mathit{ev}^{\bullet,P}
stands for one of the evaluations maps (
4.69
), (
4.70
) and (
4.71
) in (ii), and
\mathit{pr}_{i}:L^{k+1}\rightarrow L
is the projection to the
(i+1)
th factor. The signs
\varepsilon_{1},\cdots,\varepsilon_{16}
above are given as follows:

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{1}=(k_{1}-i)(k_{2}-1)+n+k_{1}-1,\\
&\varepsilon_{2}=(k_{1}-i)(k_{2}-1)+n+k,\textrm{ }\varepsilon_{3}=(k_{1}-i)(k_{2}-1)+n+1,\\
&\varepsilon_{4,j}=n+|y_{j}|,\textrm{ }0\leq j\leq l,\textrm{ }\varepsilon_{5}=0,\textrm{ }\varepsilon_{6}=0,\\
&\varepsilon_{7}=1,\textrm{ }\varepsilon_{8}=0,\textrm{ }\varepsilon_{9}=(k_{1}-i)(k_{2}-1)+n+k_{1},\\
&\varepsilon_{10}=1,\textrm{ }\varepsilon_{11}=0,\\
&\varepsilon_{12}=(k_{1}-i)(k_{2}-1)+n+k+1,\textrm{ }\varepsilon_{13}=(k_{1}-i)(k_{2}-1)+n,\\
&\varepsilon_{14,j}=n+|y_{j}|+1,\textrm{ }0\leq j\leq l,\textrm{ }\varepsilon_{15}=1,\textrm{ }\varepsilon_{16}=1,\\
\end{split}

(4.86)

The compatibility of the Kuranishi structures at the boundaries of
{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
,
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
and
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
are similar to that of
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
described above, we do not write down the details here since they will not be needed for our main argument in Section
4.5
.

(v)

(Compatibility at corners, I) Isomorphisms of admissible K-spaces (for simplicity, we do not consider orientations of the moduli spaces for these isomorphisms)

\begin{split}&\widehat{S}_{r}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;P)\cong\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T,B)\in\mathcal{G}(k+2,\beta)\\
\#C_{1,\mathit{int}}(T)+d=r\\
v_{0}\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{e\in C_{1,\mathit{int}}(T)}\widehat{S}_{d}P\times L\right)\textrm{ }{{}_{\Delta}\times\mathit{ev}_{\mathit{int}}}\textrm{ }\\
&\left(\prod_{v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\setminus\{v_{0}\}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{v}+1}\left(L,B(v);\widehat{S}_{d}P\right)\times\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{v_{0}}+1,\vartheta}\left(L,B(v_{0});\widehat{S}_{d}P\right)\right),\end{split}

(4.87)

where the fiber product on the right-hand side is taken over
\prod_{e\in C_{1,\mathit{int}}(T)}\left(\widehat{S}_{d}P\times L\right)^{2}
.

\begin{split}&\widehat{S}_{r}\left({}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\right)\cong\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T,\ring{B},v_{0})\in\mathcal{G}(k+1,\ring{\beta})\\
\#C_{1,\mathit{int}}(T)+d+r_{1}+r_{2}=r\\
v_{0}\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\end{subarray}}\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}+\cdots+j_{r_{2}}=l_{2}\\
l_{1}+l_{2}=l\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{e\in C_{1,\mathit{int}}(T)}\widehat{S}_{d}P\times L\right)\\
&\textrm{ }{{}_{\Delta}\times\mathit{ev}_{\mathit{int}}}\textrm{ }\left(\prod_{v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\setminus\{v_{0}\}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{v}+1}\left(L,\ring{B}(v);\widehat{S}_{d}P\right)\right.\\
&\left.\times\left(\prod_{i=1}^{r_{2}}{}_{j_{i}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(y_{j_{i}},x;\widehat{S}_{d}P)\times\widehat{S}_{r_{1}}({}_{l_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{v_{0}}+1}^{1})\left(y_{j_{i}},L,\ring{B}(v_{0});\widehat{S}_{d}P\right)\right)\right),\\
\end{split}

(4.88)

where
\widehat{S}_{r_{1}}({}_{l_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{v_{0}}+1}^{1})
denotes the codimension
r_{1}<r
corner of the moduli space of domains
{}_{l_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{v_{0}}+1}^{1}
, and
y_{j_{i}}
are 1-periodic orbits of
X_{H_{t}}
.
The identifications of the codimension
r
corners of the moduli spaces
{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
,
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
and
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
are similar to that of
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
above, and are therefore omitted.

(vi)

(Compatibility at corners, II) Let
X
be either
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}(L,\beta;P)
,
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
,
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;P)
,
{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
,
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
and
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
. Then for every
l,l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
, the canonical covering map
\pi_{l,l^{\prime}}:\widehat{S}_{l^{\prime}}(\widehat{S}_{l}X)\rightarrow\widehat{S}_{l+l^{\prime}}(X)
coincides with the map defined from the fiber product presentation in (v).

(vii)

(Cyclic invariance) Let
X
be one of the moduli spaces
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}(L,\beta;P)
,
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;P)
,
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
,
{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
, and
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
. Then the Kuranishi structure on
X
is invariant under the
\mathbb{Z}_{k+1}
-action induced by the cyclic permutations of boundary marked points
z_{0},\cdots,z_{k}
.

Proof.

We shall explain here some important points in the statement of the theorem which are not self-evident.

The constructions of the Kuranishi structures on the moduli spaces
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
,
{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
,
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
,
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
and
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{1}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
are combinations of the constructions of the Kuranishi structures for the moduli spaces of pseudoholomorphic discs (tree-like K-systems, cf.
[
25
,
26
]
) and that of the moduli spaces of solutions to Floer’s equation associated to a time-dependent Hamiltonian function (linear K-systems, cf.
[
24
,
27
]
). In fact, since in our case the transversality of the moduli spaces
{}_{j}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x,y)
of Floer cylinders can be achieved by carefully choosing Floer data, the constructions of Kuranishi structures on these moduli spaces are essentially reduced to that of
J_{M}
-holomorphic discs. However, as we will discuss shortly, in order to achieve the invariance property (vii), we need to be a little bit careful when constructing these Kuranishi structures. The dimension computations are straightforward. The explicit description of a Kuranishi chart of
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
can be found in Lemma
46
.

We explain how to define the evaluation map

{}_{l}\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R}}=({}_{l}\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R}}_{0},\cdots,{}_{l}\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R}}_{k}):{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})\rightarrow L^{k+1}.

(4.89)

For each
(T,\ring{B},v_{0})\in\mathcal{G}(k+1,\ring{\beta})
, each
0\leq l_{1}\leq l
, and each decomposition
l-l_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{r_{2}}j_{i}
, with
j_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
for each
1\leq i\leq r_{2}
, we can define the exterior evaluation map

\begin{split}\mathit{ev}_{\mathit{ext}}:&\prod_{v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\setminus\{v_{0}\}}\mathcal{R}_{k_{v}+1}\left(L,\ring{B}(v)\right)\times\prod_{i=1}^{r_{2}}{}_{j_{i}}\mathcal{M}(y_{j_{i}},x)\times{}_{l_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{k_{v_{0}}+1}^{1}\left(y_{j_{i}},L,\ring{B}(v_{0})\right)\rightarrow L^{k+1}\end{split}

(4.90)

in the same manner as the maps (
4.4
) and (
4.12
) in Section
4.1
. The map
{}_{l}\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R}}
is given by restricting
\mathit{ev}^{\mathit{ext}}
to the compactified moduli space

\begin{split}&{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})\cong\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T,\ring{B},v_{0})\in\mathcal{G}(k+1,\ring{\beta})\\
v_{0}\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\end{subarray}}\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}+\cdots+j_{r_{2}}=l_{2}\\
l_{1}+l_{2}=l\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{e\in C_{1,\mathit{int}}(T)}L\right)\textrm{ }{{}_{\Delta}\times\mathit{ev}_{\mathit{int}}}\textrm{ }\\
&\left(\prod_{v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\setminus\{v_{0}\}}\mathcal{R}_{k_{v}+1}\left(L,\ring{B}(v)\right)\times\prod_{i=1}^{r_{2}}{}_{j_{i}}\mathcal{M}(y_{j_{i}},x)\times{}_{l_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{k_{v_{0}}+1}^{1}\left(y_{j_{i}},L,\ring{B}(v_{0})\right)\right).\\
\end{split}

(4.91)

This definition directly extends to the moduli spaces
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
with
P
-coefficients and gives rise to the evaluation map
{}_{l}\mathit{ev}^{\mathcal{R},P}
in (ii). The constructions of the evaluation maps (
4.72
), (
4.73
) and (
4.74
) are similar, and we omit the details.

The isomorphisms (
4.82
) and (
4.84
) follow from (
4.9
). Note that in (
4.82
) and (
4.84
), there is no boundary strata of the form

\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}(L,\beta_{1};P)\textrm{ }{{}_{i}\times_{0}}\textrm{ }\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta_{2};P).

(4.92)

This is because according to the construction, the constraint imposed on the boundary marked point with “the largest label” only makes sense on the disc component with which carries
z_{0}
.

For the isomorphisms (
4.83
) and (
4.84
), note that the codimension
1
boundary strata of the compactified moduli space
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta})
are covered by the natural inclusions of

{}_{j}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x,y_{j})\times{}_{l-j}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(y_{j},L,\ring{\beta}),\textrm{ where }1\leq j\leq l,

(4.93)

{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta}),\textrm{ where }1\leq j\leq l-1,

(4.94)

{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta}),

(4.95)

which come from the degenerations of domains,

\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x,y)\times{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(y,L,\ring{\beta}),

(4.96)

which comes from semi-stable breaking, and

{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta}_{1})\textrm{ }{{}_{i}\times}_{0}\textrm{ }\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+1}(L,\beta_{2}),\textrm{ where }k_{1}+k_{2}=k+1,1\leq i\leq k_{1}\textrm{ and }\ring{\beta}_{1}+\beta_{2}=\ring{\beta},

(4.97)

\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}(L,\beta_{1})\textrm{ }{{}_{i}\times}_{0}\textrm{ }{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta}_{2}),\textrm{ where }k_{1}+k_{2}=k+1,1\leq i\leq k_{1}\textrm{ and }\beta_{1}+\ring{\beta}_{2}=\ring{\beta},

(4.98)

which are disc bubbles.

In the above, the strata (
4.93
)-(
4.95
) correspond respectively to the strata (
4.29
)-(
4.32
) in Proposition
36
. Due to the possible non-exactness of
L
in our case, there can be disc bubbles. By our choice of Floer data as specified in Definition
38
, their contributions give the boundary strata (
4.97
) and (
4.98
).

The sign computations in (iv) will be carried out in Appendix
B
. The compatibility conditions in (iv) and (v) will be explained at the end of this subsection.

For the moduli spaces
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}(L,\beta;P)
and
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;P)
, the invariance property (vii) of the Kuranishi structures follows from the construction of Fukaya (cf.
[
20
]
, Corollary 3.1). The arguments for the moduli spaces
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
,
{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
and
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
are similar. For clarity, we breifly explain here Fukaya’s construction. The key point is to observe that
[
20
]
, Lemma 3.1 holds not only for the case when the domain is a disc, but a punctured disc as well. For simplicity, we consider here only the case of
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
. Let
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
be the Cohen-Ganatra moduli space constructed as in Section
4.2
, but using the domains
(S;p_{1},\cdots,p_{l};\ell)\in{}_{l}\mathcal{R}^{1}
without boundary marked points. This enables us to construct Kuranishi structures on
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
for various
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
by pulling back the Kuranishi structure on the moduli space
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
, via the map

f_{k+1}:{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\rightarrow{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)

(4.99)

which forgets all the boundary marked points
z_{0},\cdots,z_{k}
. Here, what is slightly different from the cases of the moduli spaces
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}(L,\beta;P)
and
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;P)
is that additional care must be taken when
\partial\ring{\beta}=0
, in which case there is an extra boundary stratum in
\partial{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
corresponding to a configuration of Floer cylinders (with additional marked points) in
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x,\mathit{pt})
, where
\mathit{pt}\in L
is regarded as a constant orbit of
X_{H_{t}}
, attached to a constant disc at
\mathit{pt}
. Equivalently, denote by
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x)
the moduli space of pairs
\left((\mathbb{C},p_{1},\cdots,p_{l}),u\right)
, where
p_{1},\cdots,p_{l}\in\mathbb{C}
are auxiliary marked points satisfying

0<|p_{1}|<\cdots<|p_{l}|,

(4.100)

and
u:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow M
is a solution to the Floer equation with the asymptotic condition at the positive cylindrical end given by
x
. Evaluation at the origin
0\in\mathbb{C}
defines a map
\mathit{ev}_{0}^{\mathcal{M}}:{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x)\rightarrow M
, with which the extra boundary stratum can be written as the fiber product

{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x)\textrm{ }{{}_{\mathit{ev}_{0}^{\mathcal{M}}}\times_{M}}\textrm{ }L.

(4.101)

In this case, we need a Kuranishi structure on
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x)
which is compatible with the gluings of Floer trajectories, such that
\mathit{ev}_{0}^{\mathcal{M}}
is strongly continuous and weakly submersive. Since transversalities of (
4.101
) can be achieved by appropriately choosing Floer data, this is obviously true with trivial obstruction bundle. Taking a manifold (with corners) chart
U_{p}
of
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x)
, where
p\in{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x)
, the restriction of
\mathit{ev}_{0}^{\mathcal{M}}
defines a map
U_{p}\rightarrow M
. Define
V_{p}:=(\mathit{ev}_{0}^{\mathcal{M}}|_{U_{p}})^{-1}(L)
, then its product with the compactification of the configuration space

\mathit{Conf}_{k+1}:=\left\{(z_{0},\cdots,z_{k})\in(\partial S)^{k+1}|\textrm{the points }z_{i}\textrm{'s respect cyclic order}\right\}/S^{1}

(4.102)

of
k+1
cyclically ordered points on the circle can be regarded as a stratum of a Kuranishi neighborhood of the pullback of
(p,q)\in{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x)\textrm{ }{{}_{\mathit{ev}_{0}^{\mathcal{M}}}\times_{M}}\textrm{ }L\subset\partial{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
in
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
. We can then extend it to a Kuranishi neighborhood of
f_{k+1}^{-1}(p,q)
in
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
.
∎

Remark 45
.

Theorem
44
above is actually parallel to
[
34
]
, Theorem 7.20. A remarkable difference is that the invariance property (vii) of the Kuranishi structures for certain moduli spaces under cyclic permutations of boundary marked points is not required there. However, this is crucial for our purposes since we are working in the
S^{1}
-equivariant case, where both the
S^{1}
-equivariant differential and the chain level string bracket involve cyclic permutations of the marked points on the Moore loops. Note also that the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
does not admit a Kuranishi structure which is invariant under the cyclic permutations of the boundary marked points. Instead, by our choices of Floer data, the group
\mathbb{Z}_{k+1}
of cyclic permutations acts transitively on the set of admissible K-spaces
\left\{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\right\}_{i=0}^{k}
.

From now on, we shall fix Kuranishi structures on the moduli spaces (
4.60
)—(
4.65
) satisfying (ii)-(vii) of Theorem
44
.

We describe explicitly the Kuranishi charts of
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
. Let

{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{K}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)

(4.103)

be the space of pairs (
4.44
), where
(S;z_{0},\cdots,z_{k},p_{1},\cdots,p_{l};\ell)\in{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}
, but now
u:S\rightarrow M
is a
C^{\infty}
-map satisfying
u(\partial S)\subset L
,
[u]=\ring{\beta}\in\pi_{2}(M,x,L)
,
\bar{\partial}u=0
(with respect to
J_{M}
) in a neighborhood of
\partial S
, and

\partial_{s}u+J_{t}(\partial_{t}u-X_{H_{t}})=0

(4.104)

on the (positive) cylindrical end, if there is no cylinders breaking off at
\zeta
. When cylinders bubble off at
\zeta
,
u
is required to satisfy (
4.104
) on the cylindrical end of the main component, and solves the corresponding Floer equations of the form (
4.21
) on the cylinder components (which may carry some of the auxiliary marked points
p_{1},\cdots,p_{l}
). Moreover, we have the space

\overline{\mathcal{K}}_{k+1}(L,\beta;P)

(4.105)

introduced in
[
34
]
, which parametrizes pairs the (
4.2
), but now
u:(D,\partial D)\rightarrow(M,L)
is a
C^{\infty}
-map satisfying
\bar{\partial}u=0
on a small neighborhood of
\partial D
and
[u]=\beta\in\pi_{2}(M,L)
. Since we can take the obstruction bundles
\mathcal{E}
in both cases so that every section of it is supported away from
\partial S
(resp.
\partial D
) and the cylindrical end near
\zeta
(in fact, the punctured disc of radius
\frac{1}{2}
), the following lemma is straightforward.

Lemma 46
.

Let
p\in{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
and
\mathcal{U}_{p}=(U_{p},\mathcal{E}_{p},s_{p},\psi_{p})
be a Kuranishi chart at
p
. Let
(T,\ring{B},v_{0})\in\mathcal{G}(k+1,\ring{\beta})
,
0\leq l_{1}\leq l
,
l-l_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{r_{2}}j_{i}
, where
j_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
for each
1\leq i\leq r_{2}
, such that

\begin{split}p\in P&\times\left(\prod_{e\in C_{1,\mathit{int}}(T)}L\right)\textrm{ }{{}_{\Delta}\times\mathit{ev}_{\mathit{int}}}\textrm{ }\left(\prod_{v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T)\setminus\{v_{0}\}}\mathcal{R}_{k_{v}+1}\left(L,\ring{B}(v)\right)\right.\\
&\left.\times\prod_{i=1}^{r_{2}}{}_{j_{i}}\mathcal{M}(y_{j_{i}},x)\times{}_{l_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{k_{v_{0}}+1}^{1}\left(y_{j_{i}},L,\ring{B}(v_{0})\right)\right)\end{split}

(4.106)

Then set theoretically
U_{p}
can be embedded into

\begin{split}\bigsqcup_{(T^{\prime},B^{\prime},v_{0})}P&\times\left(\prod_{e\in C_{1,\mathit{int}}(T^{\prime})}L\right)\textrm{ }{{}_{\Delta}\times_{\mathit{ev}_{\mathit{int}}}}\textrm{ }\left(\prod_{v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T^{\prime})\setminus\{v_{0}\}}\overline{\mathcal{K}}_{k_{v}+1}\left(L,B^{\prime}(v)\right)\right.\\
&\left.\times{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{K}}^{1}_{k_{v_{0}}+1}\left(x,L,\ring{B}^{\prime}(v_{0})\right)\right),\end{split}

(4.107)

where
(T^{\prime},\ring{B}^{\prime},v_{0})
runs over all reductions of
(T,\ring{B},v_{0})
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be the collection of all the moduli spaces appeared in Theorem
44
, except for those of the form
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}};P)
. To achieve the compatibility conditions (iv)-(vi) in Theorem
44
, we need a total order on
\mathcal{C}
with the following property: if a moduli space
X\in\mathcal{C}
is part of the normalized boundary
\partial Y
of another moduli space
Y\in\mathcal{C}
, then
X<Y
. Concretely, we choose our total order on
\mathcal{C}
as follows:

(i)

X<Y
if the
P
-part of
X
is
\{m\}
and the
P
-part of
Y
is
[m^{\prime},m^{\prime}+1]
, for any
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

(ii)

X<Y
if the
P
-part of
X
is
\{m\}
and the
P
-part of
Y
is
\{m+1\}
.

(iii)

X<Y
if the
P
-part of
X
is
[m,m+1]
and the
P
-part of
Y
is
[m+1,m+2]
.

(iv)

For any
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4},k_{5},l_{1},l_{2},l_{3},l_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5}\in H_{1}(L;\mathbb{Z})
,
1\leq j\leq l_{2}-1
and 1-periodic orbits
x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{5}
of
X_{H_{t}}
,

\begin{split}{}_{l_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x_{1},x_{2};\{m\})&<\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}(L,\bar{a}_{1};\{m\})<\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+2,\vartheta}(L,\bar{a}_{2};\{m\})\\
&<{}_{l_{2}}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{3}+1}^{1}(x_{3},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{3};\{m\})<{}_{l_{3}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{4}+1}^{S^{1}}(x_{4},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{4};\{m\})\\
&<{}_{l_{4}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{5}+1}^{1}(x_{5},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{5};\{m\})\end{split}

(4.108)

(v)

If
k_{1},k_{2},l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
1\leq j_{1}\leq l_{1}-1
,
1\leq j_{2}\leq l_{2}-1
and
a_{1},a_{2}\in H_{1}(L;\mathbb{Z})
satisfy
\theta_{M}(a_{1})+(k_{1}-1)\varepsilon<\theta_{M}(a_{2})+(k_{2}-1)\varepsilon
, then

\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}(L,\bar{a}_{1};\{m\})<\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+1}(L,\bar{a}_{2};\{m\}),

(4.109)

\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+2,\vartheta}(L,\bar{a}_{1};\{m\})<\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+2,\vartheta}(L,\bar{a}_{2};\{m\}),

(4.110)

{}_{l_{1}}^{j_{1},j_{1}+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{1};\{m\})<{}_{l_{2}}^{j_{2},j_{2}+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{2};\{m\}),

(4.111)

{}_{l_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{1};\{m\})<{}_{l_{2}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{2};\{m\}),

(4.112)

{}_{l_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{1};\{m\})<{}_{l_{2}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{2};\{m\}).

(4.113)

(vi)

Let
k,l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
1\leq j_{1}\leq l_{1}-1
,
1\leq j_{2}\leq l_{2}-1
,
a\in H_{1}(L;\mathbb{Z})
and
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
,
x_{3}
,
x_{4}
be 1-periodic orbits of
X_{H_{t}}
. If
A_{H_{t}}(x_{1})<A_{H_{t}}(x_{2})
, then

{}_{l_{1}}^{j_{1},j_{1}+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x_{1},L,\ring{\bar{a}};\{m\})<{}_{l_{2}}^{j_{2},j_{2}+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x_{2},L,\ring{\bar{a}};\{m\}),

(4.114)

{}_{l_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x_{1},L,\ring{\bar{a}};\{m\})<{}_{l_{2}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x_{2},L,\ring{\bar{a}};\{m\}),

(4.115)

{}_{l_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x_{1},L,\ring{\bar{a}};\{m\})<{}_{l_{2}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x_{2},L,\ring{\bar{a}};\{m\}).

(4.116)

If
A_{H_{t}}(x_{1})-A_{H_{t}}(x_{2})<A_{H_{t}}(x_{3})-A_{H_{t}}(x_{4})
, then

{}_{l_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x_{1},x_{2})<{}_{l_{2}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x_{3},x_{4}).

(4.117)

(vii)

For
c\in\mathbb{R}
, we choose arbitrary total orders on sets

\left\{{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(x_{1},x_{2};\{m\})|A_{H_{t}}(x_{1})-A_{H_{t}}(x_{2})=c\right\},

(4.118)

\left\{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}(L,\bar{a};\{m\})|\theta_{M}(a)+(k-1)\varepsilon=c\right\},

(4.119)

\left\{\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\bar{a};\{m\})|\theta_{M}(a)+(k-1)\varepsilon=c\right\},

(4.120)

\left\{{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}};\{m\})|\theta_{M}(a)+(k-1)\varepsilon=c\right\},

(4.121)

\left\{{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}};\{m\})|\theta_{M}(a)+(k-1)\varepsilon=c\right\},

(4.122)

\left\{{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}};\{m\})|\theta_{M}(a)+(k-1)\varepsilon=c\right\}.

(4.123)

(viii)

For each
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we take a total order on the moduli spaces with
P=[m,m+1]
in a similar manner as (iv)—(vii) above.

We haven’t dealt with the moduli spaces
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}};P)
when defining the total ordering above. In this case, we have a (codimension 0) embedding of admissible K-spaces

{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}};P)\hookrightarrow{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}};P)

(4.124)

induced from the auxiliary-rescaling map (
4.36
). See the proof of Lemma
53
for details. Thus the compatibility at corners for
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}};P)
follows essentially from the compatibility at corners for
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}};P)
, which is guaranteed by the existence of the total order on
\mathcal{C}
specified above.

Remark 47
.

If we consider the subcollection
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{C}
of the moduli spaces
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{1}+1}(L,\bar{a}_{1};P)
,
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{2}+2,\vartheta}(L,\bar{a}_{2};P)
,
{}_{l_{1}}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{3}+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{3};P)
,
{}_{l_{2}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{4}+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{4};P)
and
{}_{l_{3}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k_{5}+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{5};P)
(the numbers of auxiliary marked points, boundary marked points, and the homotopy classes are allowed to vary, but the asymptotic Hamiltonian orbit
x
is fixed). We have
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}
, where
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}
) consists of those moduli spaces whose
P
-part is
\{m\}
(resp.
[m,m+1]
) for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Then it is a consequence of Gromov compactness that for each moduli space
X
in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}_{2}^{\prime}
), there are only finitely many moduli spaces in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}_{2}^{\prime}
) which are smaller than
X
. Because of this, we can assign a number
\tau(X)\in(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4})
(resp.
\tau(X)\in(\frac{3}{4},1)
) to every moduli space
X
in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}_{2}^{\prime}
), such that
\tau(Y)>\tau(X)
for
Y<X
, where
X,Y\in\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\prime}
(resp.
X,Y\in\mathcal{C}_{2}^{\prime}
). We will use these numbers in Section
4.5
.

4.4
Space of continuous loops

In this subsection, we show the existence of strongly continuous maps from the moduli spaces
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\beta;P)
,
{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
,
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
,
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
and
{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
to spaces of continuous loops with marked points, such that natural compatibility conditions are satisfied.

We start by recalling the notion of continuous loops with marked points. Let

\Pi^{\mathit{con}}:=\left\{(T,\gamma)|T\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},\gamma\in C^{0}([0,T],L)\right\}

(4.125)

be the space of continuous paths on
L
.

Fix an auxiliary Riemannian metric on
L
, let
d_{L}
be the associated distance function. Let
\rho_{L}
be a positive number which is smaller than the injectivity radius of
L
so that for any
r\in(0,\rho_{L}]
and
q\in L
, the ball centered at
q
with radius
r
is geodesically convex. The space
\Pi^{\mathit{con}}
admits a metric
d_{\Pi}
defined by

d_{\Pi}:=\left((T,\gamma),(T^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})\right):=\max\left\{|T-T^{\prime}|,\max_{0\leq s\leq 1}d_{L}\left(\gamma(sT),\gamma^{\prime}(sT^{\prime})\right)\right\}.

(4.126)

There are evaluation maps
\mathit{ev}_{0},\mathit{ev}_{1}:\Pi^{\mathit{con}}\rightarrow L
defined by evaluating at the starting and ending point of each continuous path, with which we can define the concatenation map

\begin{split}\Pi^{\mathit{con}}\textrm{ }{{}_{\mathit{ev}_{1}}\times_{\mathit{ev}_{0}}}\textrm{ }\Pi^{\mathit{con}}&\rightarrow\Pi^{\mathit{con}}\\
\left((T,\gamma),(T^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})\right)&\mapsto(T+T^{\prime},\gamma\circ\gamma^{\prime}),\end{split}

(4.127)

where
\gamma\circ\gamma^{\prime}:[0,T+T^{\prime}]\rightarrow L
is given by

\gamma\circ\gamma^{\prime}(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\gamma(t)&0\leq t\leq T,\\
\gamma^{\prime}(t-T)&T\leq t\leq T+T^{\prime}.\end{array}\right.

(4.128)

One can check that the concatenation map is continuous with respect to the metric
d_{\Pi}
(
[
34
]
, Lemma 7.25).

For every
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, the space
\mathcal{L}_{k+1}^{\mathit{con}}
of continuous loops with
k+1
marked points is the set of tuples
(T,\gamma,t_{0},\cdots,t_{k})
such that

•

T\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and
\gamma\in C^{0}([0,T],L)
with

,

•

0=t_{0}\leq t_{1}\leq\cdots\leq t_{k}\leq T
.

Clearly, we can identify
\mathcal{L}_{k+1}^{\mathit{con}}
with

\left\{(\Gamma_{0},\cdots,\Gamma_{k})\in(\Pi^{\mathit{con}})^{k+1}|\mathit{ev}_{1}(\Gamma_{i})=\mathit{ev}_{0}(\Gamma_{i=1})\textrm{ for }0\leq i\leq k+1\textrm{ and }\mathit{ev}_{1}(\Gamma_{k})=\mathit{ev}_{0}(\Gamma_{0})\right\}.

(4.129)

This identification enables us to equip

with a metric

d_{\mathcal{L}_{k+1}}\left((\Gamma_{0},\cdots,\Gamma_{k}),(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime},\cdots,\Gamma_{k}^{\prime})\right):=\max_{0\leq i\leq k}d_{\Pi}(\Gamma_{i},\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}).

(4.130)

The evaluation map
\mathit{ev}_{j}^{\mathcal{L}}:\mathcal{L}_{k+1}^{\mathit{con}}\rightarrow L
,
0\leq j\leq k+1
, and the concatenation map
\mathit{con}_{j}:\mathcal{L}_{k+1}^{\mathit{con}}\textrm{ }{{}_{\mathit{ev}_{j}^{\mathcal{L}}}\times_{\mathit{ev}_{0}^{\mathcal{L}}}}\textrm{ }\mathcal{L}_{k^{\prime}+1}^{\mathit{con}}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{k+k^{\prime}}^{\mathit{con}}
are defined in the same way as in the case of smooth loops.
\mathit{ev}_{j}^{\mathcal{L}}
is obviously continuous (with respect to the metric

), and the continuity of
\mathit{con}_{j}
follows from the continuity of (
4.127
).

For

(4.132)

Proof.

The map
\mathit{Ev}^{\mathcal{R}}
has already been defined by Irie, see
[
34
]
, Proposition 7.26. The definitions for the other maps are actually similar. Once the strongly continuous maps (
4.133
)-(
4.138
) are defined, the commutativity of the corresponding diagrams are straightforward. For completeness, we will sketch the definition of the strongly continuous map
{}_{l}\mathit{Ev}^{\mathcal{R}}:{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)\rightarrow P\times\mathcal{L}_{k+1}^{\mathit{con}}(\partial\ring{\beta})
. For each point
p\in{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)
, let
\mathcal{U}_{p}=(U_{p},\mathcal{E}_{p},s_{p},\psi_{p})
be a Kuranishi chart at
p
, we need to define a continuous map
{}_{l}\mathit{Ev}^{\mathcal{R}}_{p}:U_{p}\rightarrow P\times\mathcal{L}_{k+1}^{\mathit{con}}
, which is compatible with coordinate changes. By Lemma
46
,
x\in U_{p}
can be identified with an element of

P\times\left(\prod_{e\in C_{1,\mathit{int}}(T^{\prime})}L\right)\textrm{ }{{}_{\Delta}\times_{\mathit{ev}_{\mathit{int}}}}\textrm{ }\left(\prod_{v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T^{\prime})\setminus\{v_{0}\}}\overline{\mathcal{K}}_{k_{v}+1}\left(L,\ring{B}^{\prime}(v)\right)\times{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{K}}_{k_{v_{0}}+1}^{1}\left(x,L,\ring{B}^{\prime}(v_{0})\right)\right),

(4.141)

with
(T^{\prime},\ring{B}^{\prime},v_{0})
a reduction of
(T,\ring{B},v_{0})\in\mathcal{G}(k+1,\ring{\beta})
. Using this description, we can define an evaluation map
\mathit{ev}_{v_{0}}:{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{K}}_{k_{v_{0}}+1}^{1}\left(x,L,\ring{B}^{\prime}(v_{0})\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{k_{v_{0}}+1}^{\mathit{con}}\left(\partial\ring{B}^{\prime}(v_{0})\right)
, and an evaluation map
\mathit{ev}_{v}:\overline{\mathcal{K}}_{k_{v}+1}\left(L,\ring{B}^{\prime}(v)\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{k_{v_{0}}+1}^{\mathit{con}}\left(\partial\ring{B}^{\prime}(v_{0})\right)
for each
v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T^{\prime})\setminus\{v_{0}\}
using the length parametrization. The map
{}_{l}\mathit{Ev}_{p}^{\mathcal{R}}
is defined by taking concatenations of the images of
\mathit{ev}_{v}
for different
v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T^{\prime})
.

The compatibility of the family of maps
({}_{l}\mathit{Ev}_{p}^{\mathcal{R}})_{p\in{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{1}_{k+1}(x,L,\ring{\beta};P)}
with coordinate changes follows from similar arguments as in
[
34
]
, Lemma 7.28, except that now the reparametrization
\rho^{v_{0}}\in\mathit{Aut}(S)\cong S^{1}
for
v=v_{0}
, and
\rho^{v}\in\mathit{Aut}(D)
for
v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T^{\prime})\setminus\{v_{0}\}
.

Finally, we need to show the continuity of
{}_{l}\mathit{Ev}^{\mathcal{R}}_{p}:U_{p}\rightarrow P\times\mathcal{L}_{k+1}^{\mathit{con}}
for each
p
. Let
(x_{l})_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of points in
U_{p}
converging to
x_{\infty}\in U_{p}
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
x_{\infty}
is represented by an element of

\begin{split}P&\times\left(\prod_{e\in C_{1,\mathit{int}}(T_{\infty})}L\right)\textrm{ }{{}_{\Delta}\times_{\mathit{ev}_{\mathit{int}}}}\textrm{ }\left(\prod_{v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T_{\infty})\setminus\{v_{0}\}}\overline{\mathcal{K}}_{k_{v}+1}\left(L,\ring{B}_{\infty}(v)\right)\right.\\
&\left.\times{}_{l}\overline{\mathcal{K}}_{k_{v_{0}}+1}^{1}\left(x,L,\ring{B}_{\infty}(v_{0,\infty})\right)\right),\end{split}

(4.142)

for some
(T_{\infty},\ring{B}_{\infty},v_{0,\infty})\in\mathcal{G}(k+1,\ring{\beta})
. When
\#C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T_{\infty})=1
, this is elementary. For the general case, we can decompose the domain
S
into disjoint components connected to each other by small strips. Among these components, there is a disc component for each
v\in C_{0,\mathit{int}}(T_{\infty})\setminus\{v_{0,\infty}\}
, and a punctured disc component associated to
v_{0,\infty}
. One can then perform the previous analysis on each component and use the fact that the connecting strips can be taken to be sufficiently small to complete the proof. Details can be found in
[
34
]
, Lemma 7.30.
∎

4.5
Proof of the main theorem

We prove Theorem
29
in this subsection, therefore complete the proof of Theorem
6
, our main result of this paper.

From now on, let
M
be a Liouville manifold admitting a cyclic dilation
\tilde{b}\in\mathit{SH}_{S^{1}}^{1}(M)
, whose cochain level representative is
\tilde{\beta}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\beta_{l}\otimes u^{-l}\in\mathit{SC}_{S^{1}}^{1}(M)
, where
\beta_{l}\in\mathit{SC}^{2l+1}(M)
. Note that there are only finitely many
\beta_{l}
that are non-vanishing. By definition of a cyclic dilation, we have

B_{c}(\tilde{\beta})=e_{M}-\partial\beta_{-1}

(4.143)

for some
\beta_{-1}\in\mathit{SC}^{-1}(M)
, where
B_{c}
is the cochain level marking map

B_{c}:\mathit{SC}_{S^{1}}^{\ast}(M)\rightarrow\mathit{SC}^{\ast-1}(M),

(4.144)

and
e_{M}\in\mathit{SC}^{0}(M)
is the cochain level representative of the identity. We want to fix the cochain
x
in the definition of Cohen-Ganatra moduli spaces so that it comes from the cochains
\{\beta_{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
in the expression of
\tilde{\beta}
, together with
\beta_{-1}
. For a closed Lagrangian submanifold
L\subset M
which is oriented and
\mathit{Spin}
relative to
\alpha
, fix classes
a_{l-1}\in H_{1}(L;\mathbb{Z})
for each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that
a_{l-1}=0
if
\beta_{l-1}=0
, and
\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}a_{l-1}=a
. For
\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1}\in\pi_{2}(M,\beta_{l-1},L)
(when
\beta_{l}
consists of a linear combination of Hamiltonian orbits,
\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1}
is a linear combination of the corresponding homotopy classes) with
\partial\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1}=a_{l-1}
, and
P\in\left\{\{m\},[m,m+1]\right\}
, consider the moduli spaces

{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};P),\textrm{ where }l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.

(4.145)

Set
\ring{\bar{a}}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1}
. It follows that
\partial\ring{\bar{a}}=a
.

z_{m}(k):=\sum_{\theta_{M}(a)<(m-1-k)\varepsilon}(-1)^{n+k+1}\overline{\mathit{Ev}}_{\ast}\left(\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{1}(e_{M},L,\bar{a};\{m\})\right)\in C_{1},

(4.168)

\bar{z}_{m}(k):=-\sum_{\theta_{M}(a)<(m-1-k)\varepsilon}\overline{\mathit{Ev}}_{\ast}\left(\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{1}(e_{M},L,\bar{a};[m,m+1])\right)\in\overline{C}_{1},

(4.169)

where the chains (
4.160
) and (
4.161
) were defined in
[
34
]
, Section 7.6. In the above, the sums are taken over
a\in H_{1}(L;\mathbb{Z})
, which give the components of the chains (resp. relative chains) in
C_{\ast}(k)
(resp.
\overline{C}_{\ast}(k)
). One can further take sums over all
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
to obtain the full chains
x_{m},\cdots,\bar{z}_{m}
, or restrict to a particular
a\in H_{1}(L;\mathbb{Z})
to obtain the (relative) chains
x_{m}(a,k),\cdots,\bar{z}_{m}(a,k)
. Note that these chains give rise to chains in the quotient complexes
C_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
and
\overline{C}_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
under the natural projections
C_{\ast}\rightarrow C_{\ast}
and
\overline{C}_{\ast}\rightarrow\overline{C}_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
. By abuse of notations, we shall use the same notations to denote the corresponding chains in
C_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
and
\overline{C}_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
.

Using the chains (
4.162
)—(
4.169
) (realized as chains in
C_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
and
\overline{C}_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
), we can form the
S^{1}
-equivariant (relative) de Rham chains

\tilde{x}_{m}(k):=x_{m,0}(k)\otimes 1\in C_{-2}^{S^{1}},

(4.170)

\bar{\tilde{x}}_{m}(k):=\bar{x}_{m,0}(k)\otimes 1\in\overline{C}_{-2}^{S^{1}},

(4.171)

\tilde{y}_{m}(k,k+1):=y_{m,0}(k)\otimes 1+y_{m,1}(k+1)\otimes u^{-1}\in C_{2}^{S^{1}},

(4.172)

\bar{\tilde{y}}_{m}(k,k+1):=\bar{y}_{m,0}(k)\otimes 1+\bar{y}_{m,1}(k+1)\otimes u^{-1}\in\overline{C}_{2}^{S^{1}}

(4.173)

\tilde{z}_{m}(k):=z_{m}(k)\otimes 1\in C_{1}^{S^{1}},

(4.174)

\bar{\tilde{z}}_{m}(k):=\bar{z}_{m}(k)\otimes 1\in\overline{C}_{1}^{S^{1}}.

(4.175)

In the above definitions, we have specified the
k
-components of the
x
and
z
-type chains, but this is not the case for the
y
-type chains, where we have combined the chain
y_{m,0}
(resp.
\bar{y}_{m,0}
) over the
k
-component and the chain
y_{m,1}
(resp.
\bar{y}_{m,1}
) over the
(k+1)
-component. This is because we will be dealing with the
S^{1}
-equivariant differentials of these chains, so
\partial\left(y_{m,0}(k)\right)
(resp.
\partial\left(\bar{y}_{m,0}(k)\right)
) and
\delta_{\mathit{cyc}}\left(y_{m,1}(k+1)\right)
(resp.
\delta_{\mathit{cyc}}\left(\bar{y}_{m,1}(k+1)\right)
) both lie in the
(a,k)
-component of
C_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
(resp.
\overline{C}_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
).

For later purposes, we also introduce the following auxiliary chains (as before, they can be regarded as chains in
\overline{C}_{1}^{\mathit{nd}}
):

\bar{y}^{j,j+1}_{m}(k):=\sum_{\theta_{M}(a)<(m-k-U)\varepsilon}\sum_{l=2}^{\infty}\overline{\mathit{Ev}}_{\ast}\left({}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])\right)\in\overline{C}_{1},

(4.176)

\bar{y}_{m}^{S^{1}}(k):=\sum_{\theta_{M}(a)<(m-k-U)\varepsilon}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\overline{\mathit{Ev}}_{\ast}\left({}_{l-1}\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])\right)\in\overline{C}_{1}.

(4.177)

\bar{y}_{m,1}^{i}(k):=\sum_{\theta_{M}(a)<(m-k-U)\varepsilon}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\overline{\mathit{Ev}}_{\ast}\left({}_{l-1}\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])\right)\in\overline{C}_{1}.

(4.178)

Remark 51
.

In the definitions of the de Rham chains (
4.160
)—(
4.169
), (
4.176
), (
4.177
), and (
4.178
) above, we have omitted the differential form
\hat{\omega}\equiv 1
and CF-perturbations (compare with Theorem
77
). The superscripts and subscripts for various evaluation maps (
4.153
)—(
4.158
) have also been left out for simplicity of notations. For the precise choices of the parameters of CF-perturbations, we refer the reader to
[
34
]
, Remark 7.36.

The moduli spaces
{}_{j}\mathcal{M}(\beta_{l-1},y;P)
, where
y
is an orbit of
X_{H_{t}}
, will also play an essential role in the argument below, but we didn’t include them in the above discussions, since their transversalities can be achieved with standard inhomogeneous perturbations, therefore do not involve choices of Kuranishi structures or CF-perturbations.

To prove Theorem
29
, we start with the following observations.

Lemma 52
.

We have the following relation for (relative) de Rham chains in
\overline{C}_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
:

\bar{x}_{m}(k)=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{|\bar{x}_{m,0}|+k(i-1)}(\overline{R}_{k+1})^{i}_{\ast}\bar{x}_{m,0}(k+1)\circ_{k+2-i}\bar{e}_{L},

(4.179)

for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Proof.

It follows from Theorem
79
(its variation for admissible K-spaces and relative de Rham chains) and Theorem
81
in Appendix
A.3
that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\bar{a};[m,m+1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}(L,\bar{a};[m,m+1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{C}_{\ast}(a,k+1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{C}_{\ast}(a,k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\vartheta,i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathit{Ev}}_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathit{Ev}}_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1)^{|\bar{x}_{m,0}|+k(i-1)}\sigma_{k,k+1-i}\circ\tau_{k+1}^{i}}

(4.180)

commutes for every
a\in H_{1}(L;\mathbb{Z})
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, where the map
\phi_{\vartheta,i}
is induced from the map
(-1)^{(k+1)i}\pi_{\vartheta,i}
on domains considered in Section
4.1
, which identifies
\mathcal{R}_{k+2,\vartheta}
with an open sector of the moduli space
\mathcal{R}_{k+1}
. Here the operations
\tau_{k+1}
and
\sigma_{k,k+1-i}
are defined in Section
3.2
. It follows from Lemma
34
that the union of the images of
\phi_{\vartheta,i}
covers
\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}(L,\bar{a};[m,m+1])
up to codimension 1 strata, which do not affect the de Rham chain in the quotient complex
\overline{C}_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
defined via the pushforward
\overline{\mathit{Ev}}_{\ast}
.

It remains to explain the signs before the maps
\sigma_{k,k+1-i}\circ\tau_{k+1}^{i}
. Recall the operators
s_{1}:\overline{C}_{\ast}(k+1)\rightarrow\overline{C}_{\ast+1}(k)
and
\lambda_{k}:\overline{C}_{\ast}(k)\rightarrow\overline{C}_{\ast}(k)
from the proof of Lemma
17
. We claim that the identity

\begin{split}\bar{x}_{m}(k)&=N_{k}\circ s_{0}\left(\bar{x}_{m,0}(k+1)\right)\\
&=\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k}\lambda_{k}^{i}\right)(\lambda_{k}\circ s_{1}\circ\lambda_{k+1}^{-1})\left(\bar{x}_{m,0}(k+1)\right)\end{split}

(4.181)

holds. To see this, we need to identify the effect on the de Rham chain
\bar{x}_{m,0}(k+1)
induced by cyclically permuting the boundary marked points
z_{0},\cdots,z_{k+1}
with the algebraic operation
\lambda_{k+1}
, and identify the effect on the de Rham chain
\lambda_{k+1}^{-1}\left(\bar{x}_{m,0}(k+1)\right)
induced by forgetting the marked point
z_{1}
with the algebraic operation
s_{1}
. The former will be done in the proof of Theorem
81
, where it is shown that with our choices of Kuranishi structures and CF-perturbations on the moduli space
\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\bar{a};[m,m+1])
, changing the labeling of the boundary marked points from
z_{0},z_{1}\cdots,z_{k+1}
to
z_{k+1},z_{0},\cdots,z_{k}
has the effect of applying
(-1)^{k+1}(\overline{R}_{k+1})_{\ast}
to
\bar{x}_{m,0}(k+1)
, which is by definition
\lambda_{k+1}
. The latter follows from a comparison of the orientations of
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,f_{1}}(L,\bar{a};[m,m+1])
(the moduli space of pseudoholomorphic discs bounded by
L
with
z_{1}\in\partial D
marked as forgotten) with the fiber product
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2,\vartheta}(L,\bar{a};[m,m+1])\textrm{ }{{}_{1}\times_{0}}\textrm{ }\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{1}(e_{M},L,0;[m,m+1])
, which gives the sign
|\bar{x}_{m,0}|+1
, where
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{1}(e_{M},L,0;[m,m+1])
is the moduli space defining the unit
\bar{e}_{L}
. Now the total sign
|\bar{x}_{m,0}|+k(i-1)
is the sum of the sign
|\bar{x}_{m,0}|+1
with a sign
k+1
coming from the comparison between
\lambda_{k+1}^{-1}
and
\tau_{k+1}^{-1}
, a sign
k
from
\lambda_{k}
, and a sign
ki
from
\lambda_{k}^{i}
.
∎

We then analyze the codimension
1
boundary strata

\bigsqcup_{0\leq j\leq l}{}_{j}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(\beta_{l-1},y_{j,l};[m,m+1])\times{}_{l-j}\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{1}(y_{j,l},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1]),

(4.182)

where the
y_{j,l}
’s are 1-periodic orbits of
X_{H_{t}}
,

\bigsqcup_{1\leq j\leq l-1}{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1]),

(4.183)

and

{}_{l-1}\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])

(4.184)

of the admissible K-space
{}_{l}\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])
(cf. (
4.85
)), and identify their contributions in terms of chain level string topology operations.

Lemma 53
.

The following identities hold in
\overline{C}_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
for any
k,l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
:

\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{l}y_{j,l}=\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{l}\delta_{j}(\beta_{l-1})=e_{M},

(4.185)

\bar{y}_{m}^{j,j+1}(k)=0,

(4.186)

\bar{y}_{m}^{S^{1}}(k)=\delta_{\mathit{cyc}}\left(\bar{y}_{m,1}(k+1)\right),

(4.187)

where the
\delta_{j}
’s are structure maps on the
S^{1}
-complex
\mathit{SC}^{\ast}(M)
, with
\delta_{0}=\partial
, and
\delta_{\mathit{cyc}}
is the BV operator on the strict
S^{1}
-complex
\overline{C}_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
.

Proof.

First, observe that the moduli spaces
{}_{j}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(\beta_{l-1},y_{j,l})
in (
4.182
) are rigid due to dimension reasons. Since counting rigid elements of
{}_{j}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(\beta_{l-1},y_{j,l})
gives the image of
\beta_{l-1}
under the operator
\delta_{j}
, it follows that
\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{l}y_{j,l}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{l}\delta_{j}(\beta_{l-1})
. The
S^{1}
-equivariant cocycle condition satisfied by
\tilde{\beta}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\beta_{l}\otimes u^{-l}
implies that

\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{l-1}\delta_{j}(\beta_{l-1})=0,

(4.188)

so

\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{l}y_{j,l}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}y_{l,l}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\delta_{l}(\beta_{l-1}).

(4.189)

Note that the right-hand side gives
B_{c}(\tilde{\beta})+\partial\beta_{-1}
, which is equal to the identity
e_{M}
by (
4.143
). This proves (
4.185
).

For the second identity, observe that the forgetful map (
4.27
) induces an isomorphism

{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])\cong{}_{l-1}\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])\times S^{1}

(4.190)

as admissible K-spaces. Since the admissible map
{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\mathit{Ev}}^{\mathcal{R}}
factors as the projection onto the first factor followed by
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathit{Ev}}^{\mathcal{R}}
, and the virtual fundamental chain of the moduli space
{}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])
is product-like, with non-trivial degree in the
S^{1}
-factor, we conclude that

\overline{\mathit{Ev}}_{\ast}\left({}_{l}^{j,j+1}\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])\right)=0

(4.191)

in
\overline{C}_{1}^{\mathit{nd}}
. This proves (
4.186
).

The proof of the third identity is inspired by that of
[
28
]
, Proposition 6. We use the decomposition (up to some codimension
1
strata) of the moduli space
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}
into sectors
{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}
considered in Section
4.2
, which induces a decomposition of the moduli space
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])
into the corresponding sectors
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])
. More precisely, there is an embedding of abstract moduli spaces

\bigsqcup_{i=0}^{k}{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}\xrightarrow{\bigsqcup_{i}\pi_{f}^{i}}\bigsqcup_{i=0}^{k}{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{S_{i,i+1}^{1}}\hookrightarrow{}_{l-1}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{S^{1}},

(4.192)

which is compatible with our choices of Floer data, and covers all but a codimension
1
locus in the target (compare with Lemma
34
). Since codimension
1
strata do not affect the de Rham chain defined by the pushforward
\overline{\mathit{Ev}}_{\ast}
in the quotient complex
\overline{C}_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
, the chain defined by
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])
is a sum of the chains defined by the sectors

\bigsqcup_{i=0}^{k}{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])\xrightarrow{\cong}\bigsqcup_{i=0}^{k}{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{S_{i,i+1}^{1}}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1]),

(4.193)

where the identification is induced by the auxiliary-rescaling maps
\left\{\pi_{f}^{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{k}
. It follows that

\begin{split}\bar{y}_{m}^{S^{1}}(k)&=\sum_{\theta_{M}(a)<(m-k-U)\varepsilon}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\overline{\mathit{Ev}}_{\ast}\left({}_{l-1}\overline{\overline{\mathcal{R}}}_{k+1}^{S^{1}}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])\right)\\
&=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\sum_{\theta_{M}(a)<(m-k-U)\varepsilon}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\overline{\mathit{Ev}}_{\ast}\left({}_{l-1}\overline{\overline{{\mathcal{R}}}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])\right)\\
&=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\bar{y}_{m,1}^{i}(k).\end{split}

(4.194)

In order to deduce (
4.184
), it remains to show that

\bar{y}_{m,1}^{i}(k)=(-1)^{k(k-i)}(\overline{R}_{k+1})_{\ast}^{k+1-i}\bar{y}_{m,1}(k+1)\circ_{i+1}\bar{e}_{L}

(4.195)

in
\overline{C}_{\ast}^{\mathit{nd}}
for any
0\leq i\leq k
. By cyclically permuting the labels of the boundary marked points
z_{0},\cdots,z_{k}
for an element of the moduli space
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{i}}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])
, we can achieve that the auxiliary marked point
z_{f}\in\partial S
lies between
z_{0}
and
z_{1}
. By our choices of Floer data (cf. Section
4.2
) and CF-perturbations (cf. (i) at the beginning of this section), the chain
\bar{y}_{m,1}^{i}(k)
is obtained by applying the algebraic operator
\lambda^{-i}
to the de Rham chain
\bar{y}_{m,1}^{0}(k)
defined by the moduli space
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1,\tau_{0}}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}}_{l-1};[m,m+1])
, see our arguments for Lemma
52
and Theorem
81
. Thus it is enough to prove (
4.195
) for
i=0
. It is a principle explained in the proof of
[
28
]
, Lemma 11 that marking
z_{f}
as auxiliary is equivalent to putting it back in as an ordinary marked point and then erasing it by taking fiber product with the unit
\bar{e}_{L}
. Since treating
z_{f}
as an ordinary marked point gives the moduli space
{}_{l-1}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{k+2}^{1}(\beta_{l-1},L,\ring{\bar{a}};[m,m+1])
, rotated so that
z_{f}
now play the role of
z_{1}
under the new labeling, it follows that

\bar{y}_{m,1}^{0}(k)=(s_{1}\circ\lambda^{-1}_{k+1})\left(\bar{y}_{m,1}(k+1)\right),

(4.196)

which is exactly the identity (
4.195
) when
i=0
. The justification for signs is the same as in the proof of Lemma
52
.
∎

Remark 54
.

We describe here an alternative approach to prove the identity (
4.195
). To do this, we equip the Cohen-Ganatra moduli space
{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}})
with the Floer data so that a disc bubble develops (at the boundary point determined by the line segment connecting the origin and
p_{1}
) when
|p_{1}|\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}
. See the right-hand side of Figure
6
. This gives an alternative compactification
{}_{l}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}})
of the moduli space
{}_{l}\mathcal{R}_{k+1}^{1}(x,L,\ring{\bar{a}})
, which is also an admissible K-space. When
l=1
and
L\subset M
is exact, such a compactification is used in
[
49
]
to deduce the identity (2.14) there. When
l\geq 2
, a little bit more care must be taken when choosing the Floer data if we want to keep the splitting (
4.190
) induced by the forgetful map. Here, we choose the Floer data so that when
|p_{1}|
is sufficiently close to
\frac{1}{2}
, it creates a “neck region” with gluing parameter

r=\log\left(\frac{1-2|p_{2}|}{1-2|p_{1}|}\right).

(4.197)

In other words, when
|p_{1}|\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}
,
r\rightarrow\infty
, a disc bubble appears, but when moving towards the codimension 2 stratum corresponding to
|p_{1}|=|p_{2}|=\frac{1}{2}
, the disc bubble shrinks back to a point.

\times

\times
